Prediction Results Visualization

This tutorial consolidates the cross-validation results produced in analysis_prediction.Rmd and turns them into summary tables and publication-ready figures.

This file works only with the reduced set of 18 final models. In this workflow, the weather kernel (W) is used mainly in models M10-M18, whereas the categorical environment effect is represented separately by E. The phenomic component (P) should be interpreted with caution, because NIRS-based similarity may reflect strong physiological response to the environment in addition to potentially useful predictive structure.

1. Load packages

library(tidyverse)
library(knitr)
library(ggthemes)

2. Define the model catalog

model_catalog <- tibble(
  ModelNumber = 1:18,
  Model = sprintf("M%02d", 1:18),
  OriginalEta = c(
    "Eta7", "Eta8", "Eta9", "Eta10", "Eta11", "Eta12",
    "Eta16", "Eta17", "Eta18",
    "Eta65", "Eta66", "Eta67", "Eta68", "Eta69", "Eta70", "Eta71", "Eta72", "Eta73"
  ),
  GroupSpecific = c(
    "Linear genomic + E",
    "Linear phenomic + E",
    "Gaussian single-source + E",
    "Gaussian single-source + E",
    "Arc-cosine single-source + E",
    "Arc-cosine single-source + E",
    "Combined linear G/P + E",
    "Combined Gaussian G/P + E",
    "Combined arc-cosine G/P + E",
    "Weather + linear genomic",
    "Weather + linear phenomic",
    "Weather + linear G/P",
    "Weather + Gaussian models",
    "Weather + Gaussian models",
    "Weather + Gaussian models",
    "Weather + Arc-cosine models",
    "Weather + Arc-cosine models",
    "Weather + Arc-cosine models"
  ),
  Description = c(
    "E + G + GE",
    "E + P + PE",
    "E + GGK + GGKE",
    "E + PGK + PGKE",
    "E + GAK + GAKE",
    "E + PAK + PAKE",
    "E + G + P + GE + PE",
    "E + GGK + PGK + GGKE + PGKE",
    "E + GAK + PAK + GAKE + PAKE",
    "E + W + G + GE + GW",
    "E + W + P + PE + PW",
    "E + W + G + P + GE + PE + GW + PW",
    "E + W + GGK + GGKE + GGKW",
    "E + W + PGK + PGKE + PGKW",
    "E + W + GGK + PGK + GGKE + PGKE + GGKW + PGKW",
    "E + W + GAK + GAKE + GAKW",
    "E + W + PAK + PAKE + PAKW",
    "E + W + GAK + PAK + GAKE + PAKE + GAKW + PAKW"
  )
)

model_catalog

3. Locate the result files

results_dir <- "output/results"
figures_dir <- "output/figures"
tables_dir <- "output/tables"

dir.create(figures_dir, recursive = TRUE, showWarnings = FALSE)
dir.create(tables_dir, recursive = TRUE, showWarnings = FALSE)

all_result_files <- list.files(results_dir, pattern = "\\.csv$", full.names = TRUE)
all_result_names <- basename(all_result_files)

cv12_files <- all_result_files[grepl("^CV1_|^CV2_", all_result_names)]
cvloo_files <- all_result_files[grepl("^CV0_|^CV00_", all_result_names)]

cat("Number of CV1/CV2 files:", length(cv12_files), "\n")
Number of CV1/CV2 files: 186 
cat("Number of CV0/CV00 files:", length(cvloo_files), "\n")
Number of CV0/CV00 files: 744

4. Read and classify CV1 and CV2 files

cv12_list <- vector("list", length(cv12_files))

for (i in seq_along(cv12_files)) {
  current_file <- cv12_files[i]
  file_name <- basename(current_file)
  file_name <- sub("\\.csv$", "", file_name)
  parts <- strsplit(file_name, "_")[[1]]

  current_cv <- parts[1]
  current_trait <- parts[2]
  current_model_number <- as.numeric(gsub("Eta", "", parts[3]))
  current_rep <- as.numeric(gsub("rep", "", parts[4]))

  current_data <- readr::read_csv(current_file, show_col_types = FALSE)
  current_data$CV <- current_cv
  current_data$Trait <- current_trait
  current_data$ModelNumber <- current_model_number
  current_data$Model <- sprintf("M%02d", current_model_number)
  current_data$Rep <- current_rep

  cv12_list[[i]] <- current_data
}

cv12_raw <- bind_rows(cv12_list) %>%
  left_join(model_catalog, by = c("ModelNumber", "Model"))

head(cv12_raw)

5. Read and classify CV0 and CV00 files

cvloo_list <- vector("list", length(cvloo_files))

for (i in seq_along(cvloo_files)) {
  current_file <- cvloo_files[i]
  file_name <- basename(current_file)
  file_name <- sub("\\.csv$", "", file_name)
  parts <- strsplit(file_name, "_")[[1]]

  current_cv <- parts[1]
  current_trait <- parts[2]
  current_env_leave <- paste(parts[3], parts[4], sep = "_")
  current_model_number <- as.numeric(gsub("Eta", "", parts[5]))
  current_rep <- as.numeric(gsub("rep", "", parts[6]))

  current_data <- readr::read_csv(current_file, show_col_types = FALSE)
  current_data$CV <- current_cv
  current_data$Trait <- current_trait
  current_data$Env_leave <- current_env_leave
  current_data$ModelNumber <- current_model_number
  current_data$Model <- sprintf("M%02d", current_model_number)
  current_data$Rep <- current_rep

  cvloo_list[[i]] <- current_data
}

cvloo_raw <- bind_rows(cvloo_list) %>%
  left_join(model_catalog, by = c("ModelNumber", "Model"))

head(cvloo_raw)

6. Save the raw consolidated tables

readr::write_csv(cv12_raw, file.path(tables_dir, "prediction_cv1_cv2_raw.csv"))
readr::write_csv(cvloo_raw, file.path(tables_dir, "prediction_cv0_cv00_raw.csv"))
readr::write_csv(model_catalog, file.path(tables_dir, "prediction_model_catalog.csv"))

7. Summarize CV1 and CV2 results by repetition

For CV1 and CV2, the raw files store environment-level correlations replicated across rows. To estimate variability correctly, we first reduce the data to one weighted predictive correlation per repetition and only then compute the mean and standard deviation across repetitions.

cv12_env_rep <- cv12_raw %>%
  group_by(ModelNumber, Model, OriginalEta, GroupSpecific, Description, Trait, CV, Rep, Env) %>%
  summarise(
    EnvCorrelation = mean(unique(cor[!is.na(cor)]), na.rm = TRUE),
    ValidationCount = sum(!is.na(cor)),
    .groups = "drop"
  ) %>%
  mutate(
    EnvCorrelation = ifelse(is.nan(EnvCorrelation), NA_real_, EnvCorrelation)
  ) %>%
  filter(!is.na(EnvCorrelation), ValidationCount > 0)

cv12_rep_summary <- cv12_env_rep %>%
  group_by(ModelNumber, Model, OriginalEta, GroupSpecific, Description, Trait, CV, Rep) %>%
  summarise(
    WeightedCorrelation = weighted.mean(EnvCorrelation, w = ValidationCount, na.rm = TRUE),
    .groups = "drop"
  )

cv12_summary <- cv12_rep_summary %>%
  group_by(ModelNumber, Model, OriginalEta, GroupSpecific, Description, Trait, CV) %>%
  summarise(
    MeanCorrelation = mean(WeightedCorrelation, na.rm = TRUE),
    SDCorrelation = sd(WeightedCorrelation, na.rm = TRUE),
    NumberOfRepetitions = n(),
    .groups = "drop"
  ) %>%
  mutate(
    SDCorrelation = ifelse(is.na(SDCorrelation), 0, SDCorrelation)
  )

cv12_summary

8. Summarize CV0 and CV00 under LOEO

In the updated workflow, CV0 and CV00 are treated as leave-one-environment-out analyses. Therefore, the summary should focus on the held-out environment itself rather than on repeated folds. If the raw files still contain extra rows from older runs, we retain only the rows in which Env matches Env_leave.

cvloo_env_filtered <- cvloo_raw %>%
  mutate(Env = as.character(Env), Env_leave = as.character(Env_leave)) %>%
  filter(Env == Env_leave)

cvloo_file_summary <- cvloo_env_filtered %>%
  group_by(ModelNumber, Model, OriginalEta, GroupSpecific, Description, Trait, CV, Env_leave, Rep) %>%
  summarise(
    LOEOCorrelation = mean(unique(cor[!is.na(cor)]), na.rm = TRUE),
    ValidationCount = sum(!is.na(cor)),
    .groups = "drop"
  ) %>%
  mutate(
    LOEOCorrelation = ifelse(is.nan(LOEOCorrelation), NA_real_, LOEOCorrelation)
  ) %>%
  filter(!is.na(LOEOCorrelation), ValidationCount > 0)

cvloo_summary <- cvloo_file_summary %>%
  group_by(ModelNumber, Model, OriginalEta, GroupSpecific, Description, Trait, CV, Env_leave) %>%
  summarise(
    MeanCorrelation = mean(LOEOCorrelation, na.rm = TRUE),
    SDCorrelation = sd(LOEOCorrelation, na.rm = TRUE),
    NumberOfRuns = n(),
    .groups = "drop"
  ) %>%
  mutate(
    SDCorrelation = ifelse(is.na(SDCorrelation), 0, SDCorrelation)
  )

cvloo_summary

9. Save the summary tables

readr::write_csv(cv12_summary, file.path(tables_dir, "prediction_cv1_cv2_summary.csv"))
readr::write_csv(cvloo_summary, file.path(tables_dir, "prediction_cv0_cv00_summary.csv"))

10. Compact tables for manuscript use

These tables are rounded and ordered for easier reading in the tutorial and for direct use during manuscript revision.

cv12_table_display <- cv12_summary %>%
  arrange(Trait, CV, desc(MeanCorrelation)) %>%
  mutate(
    Mean = round(MeanCorrelation, 3),
    SD = round(SDCorrelation, 3)
  ) %>%
  select(
    Trait,
    CV,
    Model,
    GroupSpecific,
    Description,
    Mean,
    SD,
    NumberOfRepetitions
  ) %>%
  rename(
    `Model subgroup` = GroupSpecific,
    `Model description` = Description,
    `Runs` = NumberOfRepetitions
  )

cvloo_table_display <- cvloo_summary %>%
  arrange(Trait, CV, Env_leave, desc(MeanCorrelation)) %>%
  mutate(
    Mean = round(MeanCorrelation, 3),
    SD = round(SDCorrelation, 3)
  ) %>%
  select(
    Trait,
    CV,
    Env_leave,
    Model,
    GroupSpecific,
    Description,
    Mean,
    SD,
    NumberOfRuns
  ) %>%
  rename(
    `Held-out environment` = Env_leave,
    `Model subgroup` = GroupSpecific,
    `Model description` = Description,
    `Runs` = NumberOfRuns
  )

cv12_top3 <- cv12_table_display %>%
  group_by(Trait, CV) %>%
  slice_max(order_by = Mean, n = 3, with_ties = FALSE) %>%
  ungroup()

cvloo_top3 <- cvloo_table_display %>%
  group_by(Trait, CV, `Held-out environment`) %>%
  slice_max(order_by = Mean, n = 3, with_ties = FALSE) %>%
  ungroup()

readr::write_csv(cv12_table_display, file.path(tables_dir, "prediction_cv1_cv2_display_table.csv"))
readr::write_csv(cvloo_table_display, file.path(tables_dir, "prediction_cv0_cv00_display_table.csv"))
readr::write_csv(cv12_top3, file.path(tables_dir, "prediction_cv1_cv2_top3.csv"))
readr::write_csv(cvloo_top3, file.path(tables_dir, "prediction_cv0_cv00_top3.csv"))

11. Plot CV1 and CV2

cv12_plot_data <- cv12_summary
cv12_plot_data$Model <- factor(cv12_plot_data$Model, levels = sprintf("M%02d", 1:18))
cv12_plot_data$GroupSpecific <- factor(cv12_plot_data$GroupSpecific, levels = unique(model_catalog$GroupSpecific))

lower_limit_cv12 <- min(cv12_plot_data$MeanCorrelation - cv12_plot_data$SDCorrelation, na.rm = TRUE)
upper_limit_cv12 <- max(cv12_plot_data$MeanCorrelation + cv12_plot_data$SDCorrelation, na.rm = TRUE)

p_cv12 <- ggplot(cv12_plot_data, aes(x = Model, y = MeanCorrelation, fill = GroupSpecific)) +
  geom_col(width = 0.75, color = "grey30") +
  geom_errorbar(
    aes(
      ymin = MeanCorrelation - SDCorrelation,
      ymax = MeanCorrelation + SDCorrelation
    ),
    width = 0.20,
    linewidth = 0.35
  ) +
  facet_grid(Trait ~ CV) +
  geom_hline(yintercept = 0, linetype = "dashed", color = "grey40") +
  scale_y_continuous(
    name = "Predictive correlation",
    limits = c(lower_limit_cv12 - 0.03, upper_limit_cv12 + 0.03)
  ) +
  scale_x_discrete(name = "Model") +
  ggthemes::scale_fill_gdocs(name = "Model subgroup") +
  theme_bw() +
  theme(
    plot.title = element_text(face = "bold", size = 12, hjust = 0.5),
    strip.text = element_text(face = "bold", size = 10),
    axis.text.x = element_text(angle = 90, vjust = 0.5, hjust = 1, size = 8),
    axis.text.y = element_text(size = 9),
    axis.title = element_text(size = 10),
    legend.position = "top",
    legend.title = element_text(size = 9),
    legend.text = element_text(size = 8)
  ) +
  guides(fill = guide_legend(ncol = 2, byrow = TRUE)) +
  labs(title = "Predictive ability under CV1 and CV2")

p_cv12

Predictive ability of the 18 selected models under CV1 and CV2 for grain yield (GY) and kernel weight (KW). Bars represent the mean weighted predictive correlation across repetitions, and error bars represent one standard deviation across repetitions. 

ggsave(file.path(figures_dir, "prediction_cv1_cv2.tiff"), plot = p_cv12, width = 12, height = 8, dpi = 300, bg = "white")

12. Plot CV0 and CV00

cvloo_plot_data <- cvloo_summary
cvloo_plot_data$Model <- factor(cvloo_plot_data$Model, levels = sprintf("M%02d", 1:18))
cvloo_plot_data$Env_leave <- factor(cvloo_plot_data$Env_leave, levels = c("CS11_WS", "CS11_WW", "CS12_WS", "CS12_WW"))
cvloo_plot_data$GroupSpecific <- factor(cvloo_plot_data$GroupSpecific, levels = unique(model_catalog$GroupSpecific))

lower_limit_cvloo <- min(cvloo_plot_data$MeanCorrelation - cvloo_plot_data$SDCorrelation, na.rm = TRUE)
upper_limit_cvloo <- max(cvloo_plot_data$MeanCorrelation + cvloo_plot_data$SDCorrelation, na.rm = TRUE)

p_cvloo <- ggplot(cvloo_plot_data, aes(x = Model, y = MeanCorrelation, fill = GroupSpecific)) +
  geom_col(width = 0.75, color = "grey30") +
  geom_errorbar(
    aes(
      ymin = MeanCorrelation - SDCorrelation,
      ymax = MeanCorrelation + SDCorrelation
    ),
    width = 0.20,
    linewidth = 0.35,
    na.rm = TRUE
  ) +
  facet_grid(Env_leave ~ Trait + CV) +
  geom_hline(yintercept = 0, linetype = "dashed", color = "grey40") +
  scale_y_continuous(
    name = "Predictive correlation",
    limits = c(lower_limit_cvloo - 0.03, upper_limit_cvloo + 0.03)
  ) +
  scale_x_discrete(name = "Model") +
  ggthemes::scale_fill_gdocs(name = "Model subgroup") +
  theme_bw() +
  theme(
    plot.title = element_text(face = "bold", size = 12, hjust = 0.5),
    strip.text = element_text(face = "bold", size = 9),
    axis.text.x = element_text(angle = 90, vjust = 0.5, hjust = 1, size = 7),
    axis.text.y = element_text(size = 8),
    axis.title = element_text(size = 10),
    legend.position = "top",
    legend.title = element_text(size = 9),
    legend.text = element_text(size = 8)
  ) +
  guides(fill = guide_legend(ncol = 2, byrow = TRUE)) +
  labs(title = "Predictive ability under CV0 and CV00")

p_cvloo

Predictive ability of the 18 selected models under CV0 and CV00 in each held-out environment. Bars represent the mean predictive correlation for the leave-one-environment-out target, and error bars represent variation only when more than one run is available for the same target environment. 

ggsave(file.path(figures_dir, "prediction_cv0_cv00.tiff"), plot = p_cvloo, width = 14, height = 10, dpi = 300, bg = "white")

13. Final validation notes

Compared with the original analysis.Rmd, this revised visualization script makes the following points explicit:

  1. it works only with the selected 18 models, not with the full original set;
  2. it uses the reduced model numbering M01 to M18, while preserving the original Eta labels only for internal traceability;
  3. it uses more specific model subgroups instead of only three broad groups, which improves the interpretation of the final figures;
  4. it standardizes the figures with scale_*_gdocs aesthetics;
  5. it saves the final figures only in .tiff format;
  6. it treats CV1 and CV2 as repeated multi-environment prediction schemes and estimates variability from repetition-level summaries;
  7. it treats CV0 and CV00 as leave-one-environment-out analyses and summarizes the held-out environment directly;
  8. it keeps W associated mainly with models M10-M18 and therefore interprets it as a weather-derived complement to E, not as a replacement for the categorical environment effect;
  9. it highlights that strong phenomic (P) performance should be interpreted carefully, because it may reflect strong response to the environment rather than stable biological complementarity;
  10. it improves the summary tables by exporting rounded display tables and top-model tables for easier manuscript use.
